use {
    json_schema_ast::SchemaDocument,
    schemars::{
        Schema,
        transform::{
            RecursiveTransform, RemoveRefSiblings, ReplaceConstValue, ReplacePrefixItems,
            ReplaceUnevaluatedProperties, Transform,
        },
    },
    std::collections::BTreeSet,
    tracing::debug,
};

/// Prune `required` entries that reference properties not defined in `properties`.
///
/// MCP tools (e.g. Home Assistant with 80+ tools) may declare `required`
/// entries for properties defined via unsupported keywords (`dependentSchemas`,
/// `if`/`then`/`else`, `patternProperties`) that get stripped by
/// `OpenAiSchemaSubsetTransform`. Gemini strictly validates that every
/// `required` entry has a matching property and rejects the request with
/// "property is not defined" when they don't match (issue #747).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
struct PruneOrphanedRequiredTransform;

impl Transform for PruneOrphanedRequiredTransform {
    fn transform(&mut self, schema: &mut Schema) {
        let Some(obj) = schema.as_object_mut() else {
            return;
        };

        // Collect property names that have a meaningful schema.  Properties
        // with bare boolean schemas (`true`) or empty objects (`{}`) are
        // treated as undefined because:
        //  - canonicalization adds `true` (the "accept anything" schema) for
        //    orphaned `required` entries
        //  - keyword stripping can reduce a property to `{}` when all its
        //    keywords were unsupported
        // In both cases the property has no usable type information and Gemini
        // rejects it with "property is not defined" (issue #747).
        let defined_props: BTreeSet<String> = obj
            .get("properties")
            .and_then(|v| v.as_object())
            .map(|props| {
                props
                    .iter()
                    .filter(|(_, v)| {
                        v.as_bool().is_none() && !v.as_object().is_some_and(|o| o.is_empty())
                    })
                    .map(|(k, _)| k.clone())
                    .collect()
            })
            .unwrap_or_default();

        if defined_props.is_empty() {
            // No usable properties — `required` is entirely orphaned.
            obj.remove("required");
            return;
        }

        if let Some(required) = obj.get_mut("required").and_then(|v| v.as_array_mut()) {
            required.retain(|entry| {
                entry
                    .as_str()
                    .is_some_and(|name| defined_props.contains(name))
            });
        }
        // Remove `required` entirely when retain emptied it.
        if obj
            .get("required")
            .and_then(|v| v.as_array())
            .is_some_and(|a| a.is_empty())
        {
            obj.remove("required");
        }
    }
}

/// Re-infer `"type"` from `"enum"` values when canonicalization stripped it.
///
/// `json_schema_ast` canonicalization removes redundant `"type"` annotations
/// when all enum values match the declared type (`lower_enum_with_type`), and
/// converts `"type": "boolean"` → `"enum": [false, true]`
/// (`lower_boolean_and_null_types`). This is correct per JSON Schema semantics
/// but providers like Fireworks AI reject schemas without explicit `"type"`.
///
/// This transform walks every schema node and restores `"type"` when:
/// - `"enum"` is present but `"type"` is absent
/// - All non-null enum values share a single JSON type
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
struct RestoreEnumTypeTransform;

impl Transform for RestoreEnumTypeTransform {
    fn transform(&mut self, schema: &mut Schema) {
        let Some(obj) = schema.as_object_mut() else {
            return;
        };

        // Only act when `enum` is present and `type` is absent.
        if obj.contains_key("type") {
            return;
        }
        let Some(values) = obj.get("enum").and_then(|v| v.as_array()) else {
            return;
        };
        if values.is_empty() {
            return;
        }

        // Collect the distinct JSON types of non-null enum values.
        let mut types = BTreeSet::new();
        for value in values {
            match value {
                serde_json::Value::Null => {}, // ignore null for type inference
                serde_json::Value::Bool(_) => {
                    types.insert("boolean");
                },
                serde_json::Value::Number(n) => {
                    if n.is_f64() && !n.is_i64() && !n.is_u64() {
                        types.insert("number");
                    } else {
                        types.insert("integer");
                    }
                },
                serde_json::Value::String(_) => {
                    types.insert("string");
                },
                serde_json::Value::Array(_) => {
                    types.insert("array");
                },
                serde_json::Value::Object(_) => {
                    types.insert("object");
                },
            }
        }

        // In JSON Schema, "number" subsumes "integer". When both appear
        // (e.g. enum mixes 1 and 1.5), collapse to "number".
        if types.contains("integer") && types.contains("number") {
            types.remove("integer");
        }

        // Only restore when all non-null values share a single type.
        if types.len() == 1 {
            let inferred_type = types.into_iter().next().unwrap_or_default();
            obj.insert(
                "type".to_string(),
                serde_json::Value::String(inferred_type.to_string()),
            );
        }
    }
}

/// Remove empty `{}` (the JSON Schema "true" schema) from `anyOf`/`oneOf`
/// composite arrays and collapse single-variant composites inline.
///
/// Canonicalization of `not` and other negation keywords produces `{}` (the
/// "accepts anything" schema). After keyword stripping, these survive as
/// empty objects inside `anyOf`/`oneOf`, which OpenAI rejects with
/// "schema must have a 'type' key" (issue #743).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
struct SimplifyCompositeTransform;

impl Transform for SimplifyCompositeTransform {
    fn transform(&mut self, schema: &mut Schema) {
        let Some(obj) = schema.as_object_mut() else {
            return;
        };

        for keyword in ["anyOf", "oneOf", "allOf"] {
            let Some(variants) = obj.get_mut(keyword).and_then(|v| v.as_array_mut()) else {
                continue;
            };

            // Drop empty-object variants (`{}`).
            variants.retain(|v| !v.as_object().is_some_and(|o| o.is_empty()));

            if variants.len() == 1 {
                // Single variant left — inline it, replacing the composite.
                let single = variants.remove(0);
                obj.remove(keyword);
                if let serde_json::Value::Object(inner) = single {
                    for (k, v) in inner {
                        // Parent-key wins: if a key is already present (e.g. `type`
                        // from a surrounding object schema), we keep the parent value
                        // and discard the variant's. This is safe for the `not`→`{}`
                        // canonicalization pattern this transform targets.
                        obj.entry(k).or_insert(v);
                    }
                }
            } else if variants.is_empty() {
                obj.remove(keyword);
            }
        }
    }
}

const OPENAI_ALLOWED_SCHEMA_KEYWORDS: &[&str] = &[
    "$ref",
    "$defs",
    "definitions",
    "type",
    "enum",
    "title",
    "description",
    "default",
    "example",
    "examples",
    "format",
    "pattern",
    "properties",
    "required",
    "items",
    "additionalProperties",
    "anyOf",
    "oneOf",
    "allOf",
    "minimum",
    "maximum",
    "exclusiveMinimum",
    "exclusiveMaximum",
    "multipleOf",
    "minLength",
    "maxLength",
    "minItems",
    "maxItems",
    "uniqueItems",
];

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
struct OpenAiSchemaSubsetTransform;

impl Transform for OpenAiSchemaSubsetTransform {
    fn transform(&mut self, schema: &mut Schema) {
        let Some(obj) = schema.as_object_mut() else {
            return;
        };

        obj.retain(|key, _| OPENAI_ALLOWED_SCHEMA_KEYWORDS.contains(&key.as_str()));
    }
}

/// Recursively strip `$schema` from all levels of a JSON value.
///
/// `json_schema_ast` validates `$schema` at every nesting level (properties,
/// definitions, `$defs`, etc.) and rejects non-Draft-2020-12 URIs. MCP tools
/// may embed `$schema` in nested definitions (issue #760), so root-only
/// stripping is insufficient.
fn strip_schema_keyword_recursive(value: &mut serde_json::Value) {
    match value {
        serde_json::Value::Object(obj) => {
            obj.remove("$schema");
            for v in obj.values_mut() {
                strip_schema_keyword_recursive(v);
            }
        },
        serde_json::Value::Array(arr) => {
            for item in arr {
                strip_schema_keyword_recursive(item);
            }
        },
        _ => {},
    }
}

fn canonicalize_schema_for_openai_compat(schema: &serde_json::Value) -> serde_json::Value {
    // Recursively strip `$schema` so `SchemaDocument::from_json()` doesn't
    // reject non-2020-12 drafts (e.g. draft-07 from Attio MCP tools,
    // issue #743, #760). Draft-07 and 2020-12 share enough structural
    // keywords that canonicalization works; remaining differences
    // (`definitions` vs `$defs`, tuple `items` vs `prefixItems`) are
    // handled by schemars transforms downstream. `$schema` itself is later
    // stripped by `OpenAiSchemaSubsetTransform` anyway.
    let mut input = schema.clone();
    strip_schema_keyword_recursive(&mut input);

    let document = match SchemaDocument::from_json(&input) {
        Ok(document) => document,
        Err(error) => {
            debug!(
                error = %error,
                "openai tool schema failed Draft 2020-12 preflight; using raw schema for best-effort normalization"
            );
            return input;
        },
    };

    if let Err(error) = document.root() {
        debug!(
            error = %error,
            "openai tool schema failed canonical AST resolution; using raw schema for best-effort normalization"
        );
        return input;
    }

    document
        .canonical_schema_json()
        .map_or_else(
            |error| {
                debug!(
                    error = %error,
                    "openai tool schema canonicalization was unavailable; using raw schema for best-effort normalization"
                );
                input
            },
            serde_json::Value::clone,
        )
}

/// Validate and normalize a JSON Schema document into the OpenAI-compatible
/// function-calling subset via `json_schema_ast` canonicalization plus
/// recursive `schemars` transforms.
pub(crate) fn sanitize_schema_for_openai_compat(schema: &mut serde_json::Value) {
    let canonical = canonicalize_schema_for_openai_compat(schema);

    let Ok(mut transformed) = Schema::try_from(canonical.clone()) else {
        *schema = canonical;
        return;
    };
    let mut replace_const = ReplaceConstValue::default();
    replace_const.transform(&mut transformed);
    let mut replace_unevaluated_properties = ReplaceUnevaluatedProperties::default();
    replace_unevaluated_properties.transform(&mut transformed);
    let mut replace_prefix_items = ReplacePrefixItems::default();
    replace_prefix_items.transform(&mut transformed);
    let mut remove_ref_siblings = RemoveRefSiblings::default();
    remove_ref_siblings.transform(&mut transformed);
    let mut subset_transform = RecursiveTransform(OpenAiSchemaSubsetTransform);
    subset_transform.transform(&mut transformed);

    // Strip empty `{}` schemas from anyOf/oneOf (left behind by
    // canonicalization of `not` and other negation keywords) and collapse
    // single-variant composites inline (issue #743).
    let mut simplify_composite = RecursiveTransform(SimplifyCompositeTransform);
    simplify_composite.transform(&mut transformed);

    // Prune `required` entries that reference properties not defined in
    // `properties`. Keyword stripping above can remove property definitions
    // (e.g. `dependentSchemas`, `if/then/else`) while leaving their names
    // in `required`. Gemini rejects such schemas (issue #747).
    let mut prune_orphaned_required = RecursiveTransform(PruneOrphanedRequiredTransform);
    prune_orphaned_required.transform(&mut transformed);

    // Re-infer `"type"` from enum values after canonicalization stripped it.
    // Providers like Fireworks AI reject schemas without explicit type
    // annotations even when enum values unambiguously imply the type.
    let mut restore_enum_type = RecursiveTransform(RestoreEnumTypeTransform);
    restore_enum_type.transform(&mut transformed);

    *schema = transformed.to_value();
}

diff --git a/crates/providers/src/openai_compat/strict_mode.rs b/crates/providers/src/openai_compat/strict_mode.rs
index 265a1466..6d4430c3 100644
--- a/crates/providers/src/openai_compat/strict_mode.rs
+++ b/crates/providers/src/openai_compat/strict_mode.rs
@@ -5,6 +5,10 @@ use std::collections::HashSet;
 /// For schemas using `"type": "string"` (or any single type), this converts to
 /// `"type": ["string", "null"]`. For schemas using `anyOf`/`oneOf`, it appends
 /// a `{"type": "null"}` variant. Already-nullable schemas are left unchanged.
+///
+/// For enum-only schemas (no `type` key — common after canonicalization strips
+/// redundant type annotations), `null` is appended directly to the enum array
+/// so the model can omit the field (issue #712).
 fn make_nullable(schema: &mut serde_json::Value) {
     let Some(obj) = schema.as_object_mut() else {
         return;
@@ -29,6 +33,12 @@ fn make_nullable(schema: &mut serde_json::Value) {
             new_arr.push(serde_json::json!("null"));
             obj.insert("type".to_string(), serde_json::Value::Array(new_arr));
         }
+
+        // If the schema also constrains values with `enum`, add null there
+        // too. Without this the type says "nullable" but the enum doesn't
+        // include null, causing LLMs to send the literal string "null"
+        // instead of JSON null (issue #712).
+        add_null_to_enum(obj);
         return;
     }
 
@@ -43,6 +53,21 @@ fn make_nullable(schema: &mut serde_json::Value) {
             return;
         }
     }
+
+    // Enum-only schemas (no `type`, no `anyOf`/`oneOf`). Schema
+    // canonicalization can strip the redundant `"type": "string"` when an
+    // `enum` already constrains the allowed values, leaving just
+    // `{"enum": [...]}`. Appending `null` to the enum is sufficient.
+    add_null_to_enum(obj);
+}
+
+/// Append `null` to an `enum` array if present and not already included.
+fn add_null_to_enum(obj: &mut serde_json::Map<String, serde_json::Value>) {
+    if let Some(enum_values) = obj.get_mut("enum").and_then(|v| v.as_array_mut())
+        && !enum_values.iter().any(|v| v.is_null())
+    {
+        enum_values.push(serde_json::Value::Null);
+    }
 }

commit 4320903550682c1941d03f9fb55f36df9fed3823
Author: Cstewart-HC <100775598+Cstewart-HC@users.noreply.github.com>
Date:   Tue Apr 14 18:06:58 2026 -0300

    fix(providers): collapse type arrays in strict mode patching (#716)
    
    Gemini via OpenRouter rejects tool schemas with type arrays like
    type: [object, string] because Gemini cannot represent union
    types. OpenAI strict mode also requires type to be a single string.
    
    The cron tool (and others) use type arrays for fields like schedule
    and payload. When OpenRouter translates these to Gemini's
    function_declarations, the union type is lossy — properties may be
    dropped while required entries persist, causing INVALID_ARGUMENT.
    
    Fix by extending patch_schema_for_strict_mode to detect type arrays
    containing object (via new type_is_object helper) and collapse
    them to object. This triggers the existing strict mode path:
    additionalProperties: false, required rebuild from properties keys,
    and optional fields made nullable.
    
    The collapse is correct for tool calling because:
    - The object form is the intended structured shape
    - Bare strings/integers are convenience shorthand for non-tool contexts
    - OpenAI strict mode already prohibits type arrays
    
    Tests: 6 unit tests + 6 integration tests covering collapse behavior,
    nested schemas, non-object arrays (preserved), and end-to-end pipeline.

 .../providers/tests/openai_compat_type_arrays.rs   | 360 +++++++++++++++++++++
 2 files changed, 392 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

+/// Check whether a schema's `type` field designates an object.
+///
+/// Matches both `"type": "object"` and `"type": ["object", ...]`.
+/// Downstream providers like Gemini (via OpenRouter) cannot represent type
+/// arrays, and OpenAI's strict mode requires `type` to be a single string.
+fn type_is_object(obj: &serde_json::Map<String, serde_json::Value>) -> bool {
+    let Some(ty) = obj.get("type") else {
+        return false;
+    };
+    match ty {
+        serde_json::Value::String(s) => s == "object",
+        serde_json::Value::Array(arr) => arr.iter().any(|v| v.as_str() == Some("object")),
+        _ => false,
+    }
+}
+
 /// Recursively patch schema for OpenAI strict mode compliance.
 ///
 /// OpenAI's strict mode requires:
@@ -119,6 +135,14 @@ fn make_nullable(schema: &mut serde_json::Value) {
 /// model can send `null` for unused optional fields instead of fabricating
 /// placeholder values.
 ///
+/// Schemas with `type: ["object", "string", ...]` (union types) are collapsed
+/// to `type: "object"` because:
+/// - OpenAI strict mode requires `type` to be a single string
+/// - Gemini cannot represent type arrays at all
+/// - When `properties` is present, the object form is the intended shape for
+///   tool calling (bare strings/integers are convenience shorthand for non-tool
+///   contexts)
+///
 /// This function recursively patches nested objects in `properties`, array
 /// `items`, `anyOf`/`oneOf`/`allOf` variants, etc.
 pub fn patch_schema_for_strict_mode(schema: &mut serde_json::Value) {
@@ -131,8 +155,14 @@ pub fn patch_schema_for_strict_mode(schema: &mut serde_json::Value) {
     // would prevent the recursive pass from recognising nested objects).
     let mut optional_props: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
 
-    // If this is an object type, apply strict mode requirements
-    if obj.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) == Some("object") {
+    // If this is an object type, apply strict mode requirements.
+    // Also handles union types like ["object", "string"] by collapsing to "object".
+    if type_is_object(obj) {
+        // Collapse type arrays (e.g. ["object", "string"]) to "object".
+        // OpenAI strict mode and Gemini both require a single string type.
+        if obj.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_array()).is_some() {
+            obj.insert("type".to_string(), serde_json::json!("object"));
+        }
         // Add additionalProperties: false
         obj.insert("additionalProperties".to_string(), serde_json::json!(false));

commit 91e183194cdebd49cd236e364ae6f96dd01c6654
Author: Fabien Penso <gpg@pen.so>
Date:   Tue Apr 14 23:32:07 2026 +0100

    fix(providers): skip strict tool schemas for Google/Gemini via OpenRouter (#716)
    
    Google's native API rejects array-form types like ["boolean","null"]
    that OpenAI strict mode produces. Parameterize to_openai_tools() with

> AGENT

Now I understand the issue. The `91e18319` commit (between the working and broken versions) introduced strict tool schemas by default for all providers except OpenRouter and Gemini. Fireworks Kimi K2.5 router model (`accounts/fireworks/routers/kimi-k2p5-turbo`) doesn't support strict mode schemas (type arrays like `["string", "null"]`, forced `additionalProperties: false`, etc.), but it's not excluded from strict mode.

Let me fix this.

    85→    /// Returns `true` when tool schemas should use OpenAI strict mode.
    86→    ///
    87→    /// Strict mode is an OpenAI-specific feature that adds `additionalProperties:
    88→    /// false` and forces all properties into the `required` array (making
    89→    /// originally-optional ones nullable via array-form types like
    90→    /// `["boolean", "null"]`).
    91→    ///
    92→    /// The `strict_tools` config field overrides auto-detection when set.
    93→    /// When unset, providers whose backends reject array-form types default to
    94→    /// non-strict: OpenRouter (proxies to Google, Anthropic, Meta, etc.),
    95→    /// Gemini direct, and Vertex AI (`googleapis.com`).
    96→    pub(super) fn needs_strict_tools(&self) -> bool {
    97→        if let Some(explicit) = self.strict_tools_override {
    98→            return explicit;
    99→        }
   100→        if self.base_url.contains("openrouter.ai") {
   101→            return false;
   102→        }
   103→        if self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case("gemini")
   104→            || self.base_url.contains("googleapis.com")
   105→        {
   106→            return false;
   107→        }
   108→        true
   109→    }
   110→

   112→        self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case("moonshot")
   113→            || self.base_url.contains("moonshot.ai")
   114→            || self.base_url.contains("moonshot.cn")

```

> AGENT

Now I need to fix two things:

1. **`needs_strict_tools`**: Fireworks router models for Kimi don't support strict mode
2. **`requires_reasoning_content_on_tool_messages`**: Fireworks Kimi router models route to Moonshot, which needs `reasoning_content`

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/functional-shame/crates/providers/src/openai/provider/request.rs",
  "old_string": "    pub(super) fn needs_strict_tools(&self) -> bool {\n        if let Some(explicit) = self.strict_tools_override {\n            return explicit;\n        }\n        if self.base_url.contains(\"openrouter.ai\") {\n            return false;\n        }\n        if self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"gemini\")\n            || self.base_url.contains(\"googleapis.com\")\n        {\n            return false;\n        }\n        true\n    }\n\n    fn requires_reasoning_content_on_tool_messages(&self) -> bool {\n        self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"moonshot\")\n            || self.base_url.contains(\"moonshot.ai\")\n            || self.base_url.contains(\"moonshot.cn\")\n            || self.model.starts_with(\"kimi-\")\n    }",
  "new_string": "    pub(super) fn needs_strict_tools(&self) -> bool {\n        if let Some(explicit) = self.strict_tools_override {\n            return explicit;\n        }\n        if self.base_url.contains(\"openrouter.ai\") {\n            return false;\n        }\n        if self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"gemini\")\n            || self.base_url.contains(\"googleapis.com\")\n        {\n            return false;\n        }\n        // Fireworks Fire Pass router models for Kimi route to Moonshot,\n        // which rejects strict-mode schemas (type arrays, forced\n        // additionalProperties). Issue #810.\n        if self.is_fireworks_kimi_router() {\n            return false;\n        }\n        true\n    }\n\n    fn requires_reasoning_content_on_tool_messages(&self) -> bool {\n        self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"moonshot\")\n            || self.base_url.contains(\"moonshot.ai\")\n            || self.base_url.contains(\"moonshot.cn\")\n            || self.model.starts_with(\"kimi-\")\n            // Fireworks Fire Pass routes kimi-k2p5-turbo to Moonshot,\n            // which requires reasoning_content on tool-call messages.\n            || self.is_fireworks_kimi_router()\n    }\n\n    /// Detect Fireworks Fire Pass router models that route to Moonshot/Kimi.\n    ///\n    /// These models use Fireworks as a proxy but the backend is Moonshot's Kimi\n    /// API, which has different schema and message requirements than Fireworks'\n    /// native models. Pattern: `accounts/fireworks/routers/kimi-*`.\n    fn is_fireworks_kimi_router(&self) -> bool {\n        self.base_url.contains(\"fireworks.ai\")\n            && self.model.contains(\"/routers/\")\n            && self.model.contains(\"kimi\")\n    }"
}

  "new_string": "// ── Fire Pass router: Kimi K2.5 Turbo (issue #810) ──────────────────────────\n\nconst KIMI_ROUTER_MODEL: &str = \"accounts/fireworks/routers/kimi-k2p5-turbo\";\n\n/// Basic completion via Fireworks Fire Pass router to Kimi K2.5 must succeed.\n/// Regression test for issue #810 where strict mode schemas caused 400 errors.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn kimi_router_basic_completion() {\n    let p = make_provider(KIMI_ROUTER_MODEL);\n    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user(\"What is 2+2? Answer with just the number.\")];\n\n    let response = p\n        .complete(&messages, &[])\n        .await\n        .expect(\"Kimi K2.5 via Fire Pass should succeed (issue #810)\");\n\n    let text = response.text.expect(\"should have text response\");\n    assert!(text.contains('4'), \"expected '4' in response: {text:?}\");\n}\n\n/// Tool calling via Fireworks Fire Pass Kimi K2.5 must not return 400.\n/// This was the primary regression in issue #810: strict tool schemas were\n/// sent to a backend that doesn't support them.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn kimi_router_tool_call_no_400() {\n    let p = make_provider(KIMI_ROUTER_MODEL);\n    let tools = vec![weather_tool()];\n    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user(\n        \"What's the weather in Berlin? You must use the get_weather tool.\",\n    )];\n\n    let response = p\n        .complete(&messages, &tools)\n        .await\n        .expect(\"Kimi K2.5 via Fire Pass tool call should not 400 (issue #810)\");\n\n    assert!(\n        !response.tool_calls.is_empty(),\n        \"Kimi router should call tools, got text: {:?}\",\n        response.text\n    );\n    assert_eq!(response.tool_calls[0].name, \"get_weather\");\n}\n\n/// Multi-turn tool use with Kimi via Fire Pass: exercises reasoning_content\n/// serialization that Moonshot requires on assistant tool-call messages.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn kimi_router_multi_turn_tool_use() {\n    let p = make_provider(KIMI_ROUTER_MODEL);\n    let tools = vec![weather_tool()];\n\n    // Step 1: trigger tool call\n    let messages = vec![ChatMessage::user(\n        \"What's the weather in Tokyo? You must use the get_weather tool.\",\n    )];\n    let response = p\n        .complete(&messages, &tools)\n        .await\n        .expect(\"first turn should succeed\");\n\n    assert!(\n        !response.tool_calls.is_empty(),\n        \"should call get_weather, got text: {:?}\",\n        response.text\n    );\n    let tc = &response.tool_calls[0];\n\n    // Step 2: provide result\n    let messages = vec![\n        ChatMessage::user(\"What's the weather in Tokyo? You must use the get_weather tool.\"),\n        ChatMessage::assistant_with_tools(response.text.clone(), vec![ToolCall {\n            id: tc.id.clone(),\n            name: tc.name.clone(),\n            arguments: tc.arguments.clone(),\n            metadata: tc.metadata.clone(),\n        }]),\n        ChatMessage::tool(&tc.id, r#\"{\"temperature\": 28, \"condition\": \"sunny\"}\"#),\n    ];\n\n    let final_response = p\n        .complete(&messages, &tools)\n        .await\n        .expect(\"second turn with tool result should succeed (issue #810)\");\n\n    let text = final_response.text.expect(\"should have text response\");\n    assert!(!text.is_empty(), \"final response should not be empty\");\n}\n\n/// Discover new models via the Fireworks /models endpoint and compare with\n/// our static catalog.\n#[tokio::test]\n#[ignore]\nasync fn detect_new_models_via_api() {"
}

> AGENT

All 5030 tests pass. Let me provide a summary of the fix:

## Summary

**Root cause**: Commit `91e18319` (between 20260413.06 and 20260420.02) introduced strict tool schemas by default for all providers except OpenRouter and Gemini. The Fireworks Fire Pass router model `accounts/fireworks/routers/kimi-k2p5-turbo` proxies to Moonshot's Kimi API, which rejects strict mode schemas (type arrays like `["string", "null"]`, forced `additionalProperties: false`, etc.) with a 400 error.

**Fix** (`crates/providers/src/openai/provider/request.rs`):

1. Added `is_fireworks_kimi_router()` helper that detects Fire Pass router models for Kimi (pattern: URL contains `fireworks.ai` + model contains `/routers/` + model contains `kimi`)

2. `needs_strict_tools()` now returns `false` for these models, preventing the broken strict schemas from being sent

3. `requires_reasoning_content_on_tool_messages()` now returns `true` for these models, since the backend is Moonshot which requires `reasoning_content` on assistant tool-call messages (prevents 400s on multi-turn tool use)

**Tests added**:
- 5 unit tests in `request.rs` verifying detection logic for both router and native Fireworks models
- 3 integration tests in `fireworks_integration.rs` covering basic completion, tool calling, and multi-turn tool use with the Kimi router model (live API, `#[ignore]`d by default)

   111→    /// Return the reasoning effort string if configured.

   114→        self.reasoning_effort.map(|e| match e {

   116→            ReasoningEffort::Medium => "medium",
   117→            ReasoningEffort::High => "high",
   118→        })
   119→    }
   120→
   121→    /// Apply `reasoning_effort` for the **Chat Completions** API (used by
   122→    /// `complete()` and `stream_with_tools_sse()`).
   123→    ///
   124→    /// Format: `"reasoning_effort": "high"` (top-level string field).
   125→    pub(crate) fn apply_reasoning_effort_chat(&self, body: &mut serde_json::Value) {
   126→        if let Some(effort) = self.reasoning_effort_str() {
   127→            body["reasoning_effort"] = serde_json::json!(effort);
   128→        }
   129→    }
   130→
   131→    /// Apply `reasoning_effort` for the **Responses** API (used by
   132→    /// `stream_with_tools_websocket()`).
   133→    ///
   134→    /// Format: `"reasoning": { "effort": "high" }` (nested object).
   135→    pub(crate) fn apply_reasoning_effort_responses(&self, body: &mut serde_json::Value) {
   136→        if let Some(effort) = self.reasoning_effort_str() {
   137→            body["reasoning"] = serde_json::json!({ "effort": effort });
   138→        }
   139→    }
   140→
   141→    /// Build the HTTP URL for the Responses API (`/responses`).
   142→    ///
   143→    /// If the base URL already ends with `/responses`, use it as-is.
   144→    /// Otherwise derive it as a sibling of `/chat/completions`, ensuring
   145→    /// `/v1` is present — matching the normalization in
   146→    /// `responses_websocket_url`.
   147→    pub(crate) fn responses_sse_url(&self) -> String {
   148→        let base = self.base_url.trim_end_matches('/');
   149→        if base.ends_with("/responses") {
   150→            return base.to_string();
   151→        }
   152→        if let Some(prefix) = base.strip_suffix("/chat/completions") {
   153→            return format!("{prefix}/responses");
   154→        }
   155→        // Ensure /v1 is present, consistent with responses_websocket_url.
   156→        if base.ends_with("/v1") {
   157→            format!("{base}/responses")
   158→        } else {
   159→            format!("{base}/v1/responses")
   160→        }
   161→    }
   162→}

    85→    /// Returns `true` when tool schemas should use OpenAI strict mode.
    86→    ///
    87→    /// Strict mode is an OpenAI-specific feature that adds `additionalProperties:
    88→    /// false` and forces all properties into the `required` array (making
    89→    /// originally-optional ones nullable via array-form types like
    90→    /// `["boolean", "null"]`).
    91→    ///
    92→    /// The `strict_tools` config field overrides auto-detection when set.
    93→    /// When unset, providers whose backends reject array-form types default to
    94→    /// non-strict: OpenRouter (proxies to Google, Anthropic, Meta, etc.),
    95→    /// Gemini direct, and Vertex AI (`googleapis.com`).
    96→    pub(super) fn needs_strict_tools(&self) -> bool {
    97→        if let Some(explicit) = self.strict_tools_override {
    98→            return explicit;
    99→        }
   100→        if self.base_url.contains("openrouter.ai") {
   101→            return false;
   102→        }
   103→        if self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case("gemini")
   104→            || self.base_url.contains("googleapis.com")
   105→        {
   106→            return false;
   107→        }
   108→        // Fireworks Fire Pass router models for Kimi route to Moonshot,
   109→        // which rejects strict-mode schemas (type arrays, forced
   110→        // additionalProperties). Issue #810.
   111→        if self.is_fireworks_kimi_router() {
   112→            return false;
   113→        }
   114→        true
   115→    }

   122→            // Fireworks Fire Pass routes kimi-k2p5-turbo to Moonshot,
   123→            // which requires reasoning_content on tool-call messages.
   124→            || self.is_fireworks_kimi_router()

Issue #810.\n        if self.is_fireworks_kimi_router() {\n            return false;\n        }\n        true\n    }\n\n    fn requires_reasoning_content_on_tool_messages(&self) -> bool {\n        self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"moonshot\")\n            || self.base_url.contains(\"moonshot.ai\")\n            || self.base_url.contains(\"moonshot.cn\")\n            || self.model.starts_with(\"kimi-\")\n            // Fireworks Fire Pass routes kimi-k2p5-turbo to Moonshot,\n            // which requires reasoning_content on tool-call messages.\n            || self.is_fireworks_kimi_router()\n    }\n\n    /// Detect Fireworks Fire Pass router models that route to Moonshot/Kimi.\n    ///\n    /// These models use Fireworks as a proxy but the backend is Moonshot's Kimi\n    /// API, which has different schema and message requirements than Fireworks'\n    /// native models. Pattern: `accounts/fireworks/routers/kimi-*`.\n    fn is_fireworks_kimi_router(&self) -> bool {\n        self.base_url.contains(\"fireworks.ai\")\n            && self.model.contains(\"/routers/\")\n            && self.model.contains(\"kimi\")\n    }",
  "new_string": "    pub(super) fn needs_strict_tools(&self) -> bool {\n        if let Some(explicit) = self.strict_tools_override {\n            return explicit;\n        }\n        if self.base_url.contains(\"openrouter.ai\") {\n            return false;\n        }\n        if self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"gemini\")\n            || self.base_url.contains(\"googleapis.com\")\n        {\n            return false;\n        }\n        true\n    }\n\n    fn requires_reasoning_content_on_tool_messages(&self) -> bool {\n        if let Some(explicit) = self.reasoning_content_override {\n            return explicit;\n        }\n        self.provider_name.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"moonshot\")\n            || self.base_url.contains(\"moonshot.ai\")\n            || self.base_url.contains(\"moonshot.cn\")\n            || self.model.starts_with(\"kimi-\")\n    }"

> AGENT

Done. The PR at https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/pull/812 now has two commits:

1. **fix**: disables strict tools and enables reasoning_content for Fireworks Kimi router
2. **refactor**: moves the detection from runtime checks to registration-time configuration via `with_strict_tools(false)` / `with_reasoning_content(true)` — using the same pattern as the existing `strict_tools` user config

The `is_fireworks_kimi_router()` function in `model_catalogs.rs` is the single source of truth for this detection, and user config (`strict_tools = true/false` in their provider settings) still takes precedence.
